{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1787313419359,"sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"read","callID":"call-0a0e36e7-6965-4c04-aa94-418827510a88-56","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/workspaces/powerchoice/.agents/skills/frontend-design/SKILL.md","limit":80},"output":"<path>/workspaces/powerchoice/.agents/skills/frontend-design/SKILL.md</path>\n<type>file</type>\n<content>\n1: ---\n2: name: frontend-design\n3: description: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n4: license: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt\n5: ---\n6: \n7: # Frontend Design\n8: \n9: Approach this as the design lead at a small studio known for giving every client a visual identity that could not be mistaken for anyone else's. This client has already rejected proposals that felt templated, and is paying for a distinctive point of view: make deliberate, opinionated choices about palette, typography, and layout that are specific to this brief, and take one real aesthetic risk you can justify.\n10: \n11: ## Ground it in the subject\n12: \n13: If the brief does not pin down what the product or subject is, pin it yourself before designing: name one concrete subject, its audience, and the page's single job, and state your choice. If there's any information in your memory about the human's preferences, context about what they're building, or designs you've made before – use that as a hint. The subject's own world, its materials, instruments, artifacts, and vernacular, is where distinctive choices come from. Build with the brief's real content and subject matter throughout.\n14: \n15: ## Design principles\n16: \n17: For web designs, the hero is a thesis. Open with the most characteristic thing in the subject's world, in whatever form makes sense for it: a headline, an image, an animation, a live demo, an interactive moment. Be deliberate with your choice: a big number with a small label, supporting stats, and a gradient accent is the template answer, only use if that's truly the best option.\n18: \n19: Typography carries the personality of the page. Pair the display and body faces deliberately, not the same families you would reach for on any other project, and set a clear type scale with intentional weights, widths, and spacing. Make the type treatment itself a memorable part of the design, not a neutral delivery vehicle for the content.\n20: \n21: Structure is information. Structural devices, numbering, eyebrows, dividers, labels, should encode something true about the content, not decorate it. Many generic designs use numbered markers (01 / 02 / 03), but that's only appropriate if the content actually is a sequence - like a real process or a typed timeline where order carries information the reader needs. Question if choices like numbered markers actually make sense before incorporating them.\n22: \n23: Leverage motion deliberately. Think about where and if animation can serve the subject: a page-load sequence, a scroll-triggered reveal, hover micro-interactions, ambient atmosphere. An orchestrated moment usually lands harder than scattered effects; choose what the direction calls for. However, sometimes less is more, and extra animation contributes to the feeling that the design is AI-generated.\n24: \n25: Match complexity to the vision. Maximalist directions need elaborate execution; minimal directions need precision in spacing, type, and detail. Elegance is executing the chosen vision well.\n26: \n27: Consider written content carefully. Often a design brief may not contain real content, and it's up to you to come up with copy. Copy can make a design feel as templated as the design itself. See the below section on writing for more guidance.\n28: \n29: ## Process: brainstorm, explore, plan, critique, build, critique again\n30: \n31: For calibration: AI-generated design right now clusters around three looks: (1) a warm cream background (near #F4F1EA) with a high-contrast serif display and a terracotta accent; (2) a near-black background with a single bright acid-green or vermilion accent; (3) a broadsheet-style layout with hairline rules, zero border-radius, and dense newspaper-like columns. All three are legitimate for some briefs, but they are defaults rather than choices, and they appear regardless of subject. Where the brief pins down a visual direction, follow it exactly — the brief's own words always win, including when it asks for one of these looks. Where it leaves an axis free, don't spend that freedom on one of these defaults. Just like a human designer who's hired, there's often a careful balance between doing what you're good at and taking each project as a chance to experiment and learn.\n32: \n33: Work in two passes. First, brainstorm a short design plan based on the human's design brief: create a compact token system with color, type, layout, and signature. Color: describe the palette as 4–6 named hex values. Type: the typefaces for 2+ roles (a characterful display face that's used with restraint, a complementary body face, and a utility face for captions or data if needed). Layout: a layout concept, using one-sentence prose descriptions and ASCII wireframes to ideate and compare. Signature: the single unique element this page will be remembered by that embodies the brief in an appropriate way.\n34: \n35: Then review that plan against the brief before building: if any part of it reads like the generic default you would produce for any similar page (work through a similar prompt to see if you arrive somewhere similar) rather than a choice made for this specific brief — revise that part, say what you changed and why. Only after you've confirmed the relative uniqueness of your design plan should you start to write the code, following the revised plan exactly and deriving every color and type decision from it.\n36: \n37: When writing the code, be careful of structuring your CSS selector specificities. It's easy to generate CSS classes that cancel each other out (especially with a type-based selector like .section and a element-based selector like .cta). This can happen often with paddings/margins between sections.\n38: \n39: Try to do a lot of this planning and iteration in your thinking, and only show ideas to the user when you have higher confidence it'll delight them.\n40: \n41: ## Restraint and self-critique\n42: \n43: Spend your boldness in one place. Let the signature element be the one memorable thing, keep everything around it quiet and disciplined, and cut any decoration that does not serve the brief. Not taking a risk can be a risk itself! Build to a quality floor without announcing it: responsive down to mobile, visible keyboard focus, reduced motion respected. Critique your own work as you build, taking screenshots if your environment supports it – a picture is worth 1000 tokens. Consider Chanel's advice: before leaving the house, take a look in the mirror and remove one accessory. Human creators have memory and always try to do something new, so if you have a space to quickly jot down notes about what you've tried, it can help you in future passes.\n44: \n45: ## More on writing in design\n46: \n47: Words appear in a design for one reason: to make it easier to understand, and therefore easier to use. They are design material, not decoration. Bring the same intentionality to copy that you would bring to spacing and color. Before writing anything, ask what the design needs to say, and how it can best be said to help the person navigate the experience.\n48: \n49: Write from the end user's side of the screen. Name things by what people control and recognize, never by how the system is built. A person manages notifications, not webhook config. Describe what something does in plain terms rather than selling it. Being specific is always better than being clever.\n50: \n51: Use active voice as default. A control should say exactly what happens when it's used: \"Save changes,\" not \"Submit.\" An action keeps the same name through the whole flow, so the button that says \"Publish\" produces a toast that says \"Published.\" The vocabulary of an interface is the signposting for someone navigating the product. Cohesion and consistency are how people learn their way around.\n52: \n53: Treat failure and emptiness as moments for direction, not mood. Explain what went wrong and how to fix it, in the interface's voice rather than a person's. Errors don't apologize, and they are never vague about what happened. An empty screen is an invitation to act.\n54: \n55: Keep the register conversational and tuned: plain verbs, sentence case, no filler, with tone matched to the brand and the audience. Let each element do exactly one job. A label labels, an example demonstrates, and nothing quietly does double duty.\n\n(End of file - total 55 lines)\n</content>","metadata":{"preview":"---\nname: frontend-design\ndescription: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\nlicense: Complete terms in LICENSE.txt\n---\n\n# Frontend Design\n\nApproach this as the design lead at a small studio known for giving every client a visual identity that could not be mistaken for anyone else's. This client has already rejected proposals that felt templated, and is paying for a distinctive point of view: make deliberate, opinionated choices about palette, typography, and layout that are specific to this brief, and take one real aesthetic risk you can justify.\n\n## Ground it in the subject\n\nIf the brief does not pin down what the product or subject is, pin it yourself before designing: name one concrete subject, its audience, and the page's single job, and state your choice. If there's any information in your memory about the human's preferences, context about what they're building, or designs you've made before – use that as a hint. The subject's own world, its materials, instruments, artifacts, and vernacular, is where distinctive choices come from. Build with the brief's real content and subject matter throughout.\n\n## Design principles\n\nFor web designs, the hero is a thesis. Open with the most characteristic thing in the subject's world, in whatever form makes sense for it: a headline, an image, an animation, a live demo, an interactive moment. Be deliberate with your choice: a big number with a small label, supporting stats, and a gradient accent is the template answer, only use if that's truly the best option.\n\nTypography carries the personality of the page. Pair the display and body faces deliberately, not the same families you would reach for on any other project, and set a clear type scale with intentional weights, widths, and spacing. Make the type treatment itself a memorable part of the design, not a neutral delivery vehicle for the content.\n","truncated":false,"loaded":[],"display":{"type":"file","path":"/workspaces/powerchoice/.agents/skills/frontend-design/SKILL.md","text":"---\nname: frontend-design\ndescription: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. This client has already rejected proposals that felt templated, and is paying for a distinctive point of view: make deliberate, opinionated choices about palette, typography, and layout that are specific to this brief, and take one real aesthetic risk you can justify.\n\n## Ground it in the subject\n\nIf the brief does not pin down what the product or subject is, pin it yourself before designing: name one concrete subject, its audience, and the page's single job, and state your choice. If there's any information in your memory about the human's preferences, context about what they're building, or designs you've made before – use that as a hint. Signature: the single unique element this page will be remembered by that embodies the brief in an appropriate way.\n\nThen review that plan against the brief before building: if any part of it reads like the generic default you would produce for any similar page (work through a similar prompt to see if you arrive somewhere similar) rather than a choice made for this specific brief — revise that part, say what you changed and why.   return 0.5 * kl_divergence(p, m) + 0.5 * kl_divergence(q, m)\n\n\ndef _js_agreement(dists: list[dict[int, float]]) -> float:\n    \"\"\"1 − mean pairwise JS / ln2, clamped to [0, 1].\"\"\"\n    if 'ford_johnson' : 'merge_sort'\n}\n\nexport const DEFAULT_QUESTIONS_PER_GROUP = 20\n\nexport function fordJohnsonBudget(n: number): number {\n  if (n <= 1) return 0\n  let total = 0\n  for (let i = 1; i <= n; i++) total += Math.ceil(Math.log2((3 * i) / 4))\n  return total\n}\n\nexport function maxUniquePairs(n: number): number {\n  if (n < 2) return 0\n  return (n * (n - 1)) >> 1\n}\n\nexport function questionBudgetBounds(n: number): { min: number; max: number; estimate: number } {\n  const estimate = fordJohnsonBudget(n)\n  if (estimate <= 0) return { min: 0, max: 0, estimate: 0 }\n  const pairCap = maxUniquePairs(n)\n  const min = Math.max(1, Math.min(pairCap, Math.ceil(estimate / 2)))\n  const max = Math.max(min, Math.min(pairCap, estimate * 2))\n  return { min, max, estimate }\n}\n\nexport function clampMinExpectedPasses(value: unknown): number {\n  const raw = Number(value)\n  const v = Number.isFinite(raw) ? Math.trunc(raw) : 2\n  return Math.min(5, Math.max(1, v))\n}\n\nexport function clampMaxRecommendedPasses(value: unknown, minPasses: unknown): number {\n  const lo = clampMinExpectedPasses(minPasses)\n  const raw = Number(value)","lineStart":1,"lineEnd":80,"totalLines":490,"truncated":true}},"title":"client/utils/sortCompare.ts","time":{"start":1787313476720,"end":1787313477061}},"id":"prt_0242f53e9001nZ6fJH6QACfTcc","sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","messageID":"msg_0242e7f020019S4ot903otKTvX"}}

{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1787313766807,"sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"write","callID":"call-8bc0100c-70ba-4a7b-91b2-13f7f24f1820-81","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/workspaces/powerchoice/client/utils/rankingConfidence.ts","content":"/** Display-only session ranking confidence from this participant’s pairings. */\n\nimport { fordJohnsonBudget } from './sortCompare.ts'\nimport {\n  fitProvisionalScores,\n  rankOrderFromAnswers,\n  type PairAnswer,\n  type RankingTarget,\n  type RankingTargetMode,\n} from './pairScheduler.ts'\n\nexport const CONFIDENCE_LEVELS = ['none', 'low', 'moderate', 'fair', 'good', 'strong'] as const\nexport type ConfidenceLevel = (typeof CONFIDENCE_LEVELS)[number]\n\nexport const CONFIDENCE_LABELS: Record<ConfidenceLevel, string> = {\n  none: 'None',\n  low: 'Low',\n  moderate: 'Moderate',\n  fair: 'Fair',\n  good: 'Good',\n  strong: 'Strong',\n}\n\nconst COVERAGE_W = 0.7\nconst CONSISTENCY_W = 0.2\nconst DECISIVENESS_W = 0.1\nconst LN2 = Math.log(2)\n\nexport type RankingEvidencePairing = PairAnswer | Record<string, unknown>\n\nexport type RankingEvidenceGroup = {\n  group_type: string\n  criterion_id?: number | null\n  pass_index?: number\n  item_ids?: number[]\n  pairings?: RankingEvidencePairing[]\n  question_budget?: number\n  ranking_target?: string\n  top_n?: number | null\n  client_group_id?: string | null\n}\n\nexport type RankingConfidence = {\n  value: number\n  pct: number\n  level: ConfidenceLevel\n  label: string\n  color: string\n}\n\nexport type RankingConfidenceInput = {\n  evidence?: RankingEvidenceGroup[]\n  optionIds?: number[]\n  factorIds?: number[]\n  defaultTarget?: RankingTarget\n}\n\ntype ChannelKey = { group_type: string; criterion_id: number | null }\n\nfunction clamp01(n: number): number {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(n)) return 0\n  return Math.max(0, Math.min(1, n))\n}\n\nfunction uniqueIds(ids: Array<number | null | undefined>): number[] {\n  const out: number[] = []\n  const seen = new Set<number>()\n  for (const raw of ids) {\n    const n = Number(raw)\n    if (!Number.isFinite(n) || n === 0 || seen.has(n)) continue\n    seen.add(n)\n    out.push(n)\n  }\n  return out\n}\n\nfunction pairKey(a: number, b: number): string {\n  return a < b ? `${a},${b}` : `${b},${a}`\n}\n\nfunction asInt(v: unknown): number | null {\n  const n = Number(v)\n  return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : null\n}\n\nexport function pairingToAnswer(p: RankingEvidencePairing | null | undefined): PairAnswer | null {\n  if (!p || typeof p !== 'object') return null\n  const rec = p as Record<string, unknown>\n  if (typeof rec.itemA === 'number' && typeof rec.itemB === 'number') {\n    const itemA = Number(rec.itemA)\n    const itemB = Number(rec.itemB)\n    if (!Number.isFinite(itemA) || !Number.isFinite(itemB) || itemA === itemB) return null\n    const response = String(rec.response || 'winner')\n    return {\n      itemA,\n      itemB,\n      winnerId: rec.winnerId == null ? null : Number(rec.winnerId),\n      response: response as PairAnswer['response'],\n      presentedLeftId: Number(rec.presentedLeftId ?? itemA),\n      presentedRightId: Number(rec.presentedRightId ?? itemB),\n      decisionSeconds: Number(rec.decisionSeconds ?? 0) || 0,\n    }\n  }\n  const left = asInt(rec.presented_left_id ?? rec.presentedLeftId ?? rec.item_a_id ?? rec.winner_id)\n  const right = asInt(rec.presented_right_id ?? rec.presentedRightId ?? rec.item_b_id ?? rec.loser_id)\n  const a = asInt(rec.item_a_id ?? rec.winner_id ?? left)\n  const b = asInt(rec.item_b_id ?? rec.loser_id ?? right)\n  if (a == null || b == null || a === b) return null\n  const response = String(rec.response || 'winner') as PairAnswer['response']\n  const winnerRaw = rec.winner_id != null ? Number(rec.winner_id) : (response === 'winner' ? a : null)\n  return {\n    itemA: a,\n    itemB: b,\n    winnerId: Number.isFinite(winnerRaw as number) ? Number(winnerRaw) : null,\n    response,\n    presentedLeftId: left ?? a,\n    presentedRightId: right ?? b,\n    decisionSeconds: Number(rec.decision_seconds ?? rec.decisionSeconds ?? 0) || 0,\n  }\n}\n\nfunction answersOf(pairings: RankingEvidencePairing[] | undefined): PairAnswer[] {\n  const out: PairAnswer[] = []\n  for (const p of pairings || []) {\n    const ans = pairingToAnswer(p)\n    if (ans) out.push(ans)\n  }\n  return out\n}\n\nfunction isDecisive(ans: PairAnswer): boolean {\n  return ans.response === 'winner' || ans.response === 'tie'\n}\n\nfunction channelId(cid: unknown): number | null {\n  if (cid == null || cid === '') return null\n  const n = Number(cid)\n  return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : null\n}\n\nfunction sameChannel(a: ChannelKey, b: ChannelKey): boolean {\n  return a.group_type === b.group_type && a.criterion_id === b.criterion_id\n}\n\nfunction targetFromGroup(g: RankingEvidenceGroup, n: number, fallback?: RankingTarget): RankingTarget {\n  const mode = String(g.ranking_target || fallback?.mode || 'full')\n  if (mode === 'winner') return { mode: 'winner', n: 1 }\n  if (mode === 'top_n') {\n    const top = Number(g.top_n || fallback?.n || Math.floor(n / 2) || 1)\n    return { mode: 'top_n', n: Math.max(1, top) }\n  }\n  return { mode: 'full' }\n}\n\nfunction coverageScore(answers: PairAnswer[], pairsNeeded: number): number {\n  if (pairsNeeded <= 0) return 0\n  const seen = new Set<string>()\n  for (const ans of answers) {\n    if (!isDecisive(ans)) continue\n    seen.add(pairKey(ans.itemA, ans.itemB))\n  }\n  return clamp01(seen.size / pairsNeeded)\n}\n\nfunction repeatedPairAgreement(answers: PairAnswer[]): number | null {\n  const buckets = new Map<string, string[]>()\n  for (const ans of answers) {\n    if (!isDecisive(ans)) continue\n    const key = pairKey(ans.itemA, ans.itemB)\n    let outcome = 'tie'\n    if (ans.response === 'winner' && ans.winnerId != null) {\n      outcome = String(ans.winnerId)\n    }\n    const list = buckets.get(key) || []\n    list.push(outcome)\n    buckets.set(key, list)\n  }\n  const repeats = [...buckets.values()].filter(list => list.length >= 2)\n  if (!repeats.length) return null\n  let sum = 0\n  for (const list of repeats) {\n    const counts = new Map<string, number>()\n    for (const o of list) counts.set(o, (counts.get(o) || 0) + 1)\n    const majority = Math.max(...counts.values())\n    sum += majority / list.length\n  }\n  return clamp01(sum / repeats.length)\n}\n\nfunction preferenceMass(order: number[]): Map<number, number> {\n  const n = order.length\n  const raw = new Map<number, number>()\n  let total = 0\n  order.forEach((id, i) => {\n    const w = Math.max(1e-12, n - i)\n    raw.set(id, w)\n    total += w\n  })\n  const out = new Map<number, number>()\n  const denom = total || 1\n  for (const [id, w] of raw) out.set(id, w / denom)\n  return out\n}\n\nfunction kl(p: Map<number, number>, q: Map<number, number>, keys: number[]): number {\n  const eps = 1e-12\n  let sp = 0\n  let sq = 0\n  const ps: number[] = []\n  const qs: number[] = []\n  for (const k of keys) {\n    const a = (p.get(k) || 0) + eps\n    const b = (q.get(k) || 0) + eps\n    ps.push(a)\n    qs.push(b)\n    sp += a\n    sq += b\n  }\n  let s = 0\n  for (let i = 0; i < keys.length; i++) {\n    const a = ps[i] / sp\n    const b = qs[i] / sq\n    s += a * Math.log(a / b)\n  }\n  return s\n}\n\nfunction jsAgreement(orders: number[][]): number {\n  if (orders.length < 2) return 0\n  const dists = orders.filter(o => o.length).map(preferenceMass)\n  if (dists.length < 2) return 0\n  const keys = [...new Set(dists.flatMap(d => [...d.keys()]))]\n  if (!keys.length) return 0\n  const divs: number[] = []\n  for (let i = 0; i < dists.length; i++) {\n    for (let j = i + 1; j < dists.length; j++) {\n      const m = new Map<number, number>()\n      for (const k of keys) m.set(k, 0.5 * ((dists[i].get(k) || 0) + (dists[j].get(k) || 0)))\n      divs.push(0.5 * kl(dists[i], m, keys) + 0.5 * kl(dists[j], m, keys))\n    }\n  }\n  const mean = divs.length ? divs.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / divs.length : 0\n  return clamp01(1 - mean / LN2)\n}\n\nfunction consistencyScore(groups: RankingEvidenceGroup[], items: number[]): number | null {\n  const allAnswers = groups.flatMap(g => answersOf(g.pairings))\n  const repeated = repeatedPairAgreement(allAnswers)\n  if (repeated != null) return repeated\n  const byPass = new Map<number, PairAnswer[]>()\n  for (const g of groups) {\n    const pass = Math.max(1, Number(g.pass_index || 1))\n    const list = byPass.get(pass) || []\n    list.push(...answersOf(g.pairings))\n    byPass.set(pass, list)\n  }\n  if (byPass.size < 2) return null\n  const orders: number[][] = []\n  for (const answers of byPass.values()) {\n    const usable = answers.filter(isDecisive)\n    if (!usable.length) continue\n    orders.push(rankOrderFromAnswers(items, answers))\n  }\n  if (orders.length < 2) return null\n  return jsAgreement(orders)\n}\n\nfunction decisivenessScore(items: number[], answers: PairAnswer[], target: RankingTarget): number {\n  const usable = answers.filter(isDecisive)\n  if (!usable.length || items.length < 2) return 0\n  const scores = fitProvisionalScores(items, answers)\n  const ordered = [...items].sort((a, b) => {\n    const d = (scores.get(b) || 0) - (scores.get(a) || 0)\n    return d !== 0 ? d : a - b\n  })\n  let hi = 0\n  let lo = 1\n  if (target.mode === 'top_n') {\n    const n = Math.max(1, Math.min(target.n || 1, ordered.length - 1))\n    hi = n - 1\n    lo = n\n  }\n  const gap = Math.abs((scores.get(ordered[hi]) || 0) - (scores.get(ordered[lo]) || 0))\n  return clamp01(1 - Math.exp(-gap))\n}\n\nfunction blendChannel(coverage: number, consistency: number | null, decisiveness: number): number {\n  if (consistency == null) {\n    const denom = COVERAGE_W + DECISIVENESS_W\n    return clamp01((COVERAGE_W * coverage + DECISIVENESS_W * decisiveness) / denom)\n  }\n  return clamp01(COVERAGE_W * coverage + CONSISTENCY_W * consistency + DECISIVENESS_W * decisiveness)\n}\n\nfunction expectedChannels(optionIds: number[], factorIds: number[], evidence: RankingEvidenceGroup[]): ChannelKey[] {\n  if (factorIds.length >= 2) {\n    return [\n      ...factorIds.map(fid => ({ group_type: 'alternative', criterion_id: fid })),\n      { group_type: 'criteria', criterion_id: null },\n    ]\n  }\n  if (factorIds.length === 1) {\n    return [{ group_type: 'alternative', criterion_id: factorIds[0] }]\n  }\n  if (optionIds.length >= 2) {\n    return [{ group_type: 'alternative', criterion_id: null }]\n  }\n  const seen = new Map<string, ChannelKey>()\n  for (const g of evidence) {\n    const key: ChannelKey = {\n      group_type: String(g.group_type || 'alternative'),\n      criterion_id: channelId(g.criterion_id),\n    }\n    seen.set(`${key.group_type}:${key.criterion_id ?? 'n'}`, key)\n  }\n  return [...seen.values()]\n}\n\nfunction pairsNeededFor(groups: RankingEvidenceGroup[], items: number[]): number {\n  const budgets = groups.map(g => Number(g.question_budget || 0)).filter(n => n > 0)\n  if (budgets.length) {\n    const minPass = Math.min(...groups.map(g => Math.max(1, Number(g.pass_index || 1))))\n    const firstPass = groups.filter(g => Math.max(1, Number(g.pass_index || 1)) === minPass)\n    const sum = firstPass.reduce((s, g) => s + Math.max(0, Number(g.question_budget || 0)), 0)\n    if (sum > 0) return sum\n    return Math.max(...budgets)\n  }\n  return Math.max(1, fordJohnsonBudget(items.length))\n}\n\nfunction scoreChannel(\n  channel: ChannelKey,\n  groups: RankingEvidenceGroup[],\n  fallbackTarget?: RankingTarget,\n): number {\n  const items = uniqueIds(groups.flatMap(g => g.item_ids || []))\n  const answers = groups.flatMap(g => answersOf(g.pairings))\n  if (items.length < 2 && !answers.length) return 0\n  const target = groups.length ? targetFromGroup(groups[0], items.length, fallbackTarget) : (fallbackTarget || { mode: 'full' as RankingTargetMode })\n  const needed = pairsNeededFor(groups, items)\n  const coverage = coverageScore(answers, needed)\n  const consistency = consistencyScore(groups, items)\n  const decisiveness = decisivenessScore(items.length ? items : uniqueIds(answers.flatMap(a => [a.itemA, a.itemB])), answers, target)\n  return blendChannel(coverage, consistency, decisiveness)\n}\n\nexport function confidenceLevelFromValue(value: number): ConfidenceLevel {\n  const v = clamp01(value)\n  if (v <= 0) return 'none'\n  if (v <= 0.2) return 'low'\n  if (v <= 0.4) return 'moderate'\n  if (v <= 0.6) return 'fair'\n  if (v <= 0.8) return 'good'\n  return 'strong'\n}\n\nexport function interpolateConfidenceColor(value: number): string {\n  const t = clamp01(value)\n  if (t <= 0.5) {\n    const warning = Math.round(t * 2 * 100)\n    return `color-mix(in srgb, var(--pc-danger) ${100 - warning}%, var(--pc-warning) ${warning}%)`\n  }\n  const success = Math.round((t - 0.5) * 2 * 100)\n  return `color-mix(in srgb, var(--pc-warning) ${100 - success}%, var(--pc-success) ${success}%)`\n}\n\nexport function emptyRankingConfidence(): RankingConfidence {\n  return {\n    value: 0,\n    pct: 0,\n    level: 'none',\n    label: CONFIDENCE_LABELS.none,\n    color: interpolateConfidenceColor(0),\n  }\n}\n\nexport function evidenceGroupKey(g: RankingEvidenceGroup): string {\n  const token = String(g.client_group_id || '').trim()\n  if (token) return `token:${token}`\n  const cid = g.criterion_id == null ? 'n' : String(g.criterion_id)\n  const ids = uniqueIds(g.item_ids || []).slice().sort((a, b) => a - b).join(',')\n  return `${g.group_type}|${cid}|${Math.max(1, Number(g.pass_index || 1))}|${ids}`\n}\n\nexport function mergeLiveEvidence(\n  evidence: RankingEvidenceGroup[],\n  live: RankingEvidenceGroup | null | undefined,\n): RankingEvidenceGroup[] {\n  if (!live) return evidence\n  const key = evidenceGroupKey(live)\n  return [...evidence.filter(g => evidenceGroupKey(g) !== key), live]\n}\n\nexport function evidenceFromLiveGroup(\n  group: {\n    client_group_id?: string\n    group_token?: string\n    group_type?: string\n    criterion_id?: number | null\n    pass_index?: number\n    item_ids?: number[]\n    items?: Array<{ id: number }>\n    question_budget?: number\n    estimated_comparisons?: number\n    ranking_target?: string\n    top_n?: number | null\n  } | null | undefined,\n  pairings: RankingEvidencePairing[] | undefined,\n): RankingEvidenceGroup | null {\n  if (!group) return null\n  const itemIds = uniqueIds(\n    (group.item_ids?.length ? group.item_ids : (group.items || []).map(i => i.id)),\n  )\n  return {\n    group_type: String(group.group_type || 'alternative'),\n    criterion_id: channelId(group.criterion_id),\n    pass_index: Math.max(1, Number(group.pass_index || 1)),\n    item_ids: itemIds,\n    pairings: pairings || [],\n    question_budget: Number(group.question_budget || group.estimated_comparisons || 0),\n    ranking_target: group.ranking_target,\n    top_n: group.top_n ?? null,\n    client_group_id: String(group.group_token || group.client_group_id || '') || null,\n  }\n}\n\nexport function computeRankingConfidence(input: RankingConfidenceInput = {}): RankingConfidence {\n  const evidence = Array.isArray(input.evidence) ? input.evidence : []\n  const optionIds = uniqueIds(input.optionIds || [])\n  const factorIds = uniqueIds(input.factorIds || [])\n  const channels = expectedChannels(optionIds, factorIds, evidence)\n  if (!channels.length) return emptyRankingConfidence()\n  const scores = channels.map((ch) => {\n    const groups = evidence.filter(g => sameChannel(\n      { group_type: String(g.group_type || 'alternative'), criterion_id: channelId(g.criterion_id) },\n      ch,\n    ))\n    return scoreChannel(ch, groups, input.defaultTarget)\n  })\n  const value = clamp01(scores.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) / scores.length)\n  const level = confidenceLevelFromValue(value)\n  return {\n    value,\n    pct: Math.round(value * 100),\n    level,\n    label: CONFIDENCE_LABELS[level],\n    color: interpolateConfidenceColor(value),\n  }\n}\n"},"output":"Wrote file successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"filepath":"/workspaces/powerchoice/client/utils/rankingConfidence.ts","exists":false,"truncated":false},"title":"client/utils/rankingConfidence.ts","time":{"start":1787313766638,"end":1787313766798}},"id":"prt_0243385120015vyMAbwxBkutco","sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","messageID":"msg_02432a9a6001Rl7b09RobsLcXO"}}
{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1787313772335,"sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"write","callID":"call-8bc0100c-70ba-4a7b-91b2-13f7f24f1820-82","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/workspaces/powerchoice/client/utils/rankingConfidence.test.ts","content":"import { test } from 'node:test'\nimport assert from 'node:assert/strict'\nimport {\n  computeRankingConfidence,\n  confidenceLevelFromValue,\n  emptyRankingConfidence,\n  interpolateConfidenceColor,\n  mergeLiveEvidence,\n  type RankingEvidenceGroup,\n} from './rankingConfidence.ts'\n\nfunction winner(a: number, b: number) {\n  return {\n    item_a_id: a,\n    item_b_id: b,\n    winner_id: a,\n    loser_id: b,\n    response: 'winner',\n    presented_left_id: a,\n    presented_right_id: b,\n  }\n}\n\nfunction unsure(a: number, b: number) {\n  return {\n    item_a_id: a,\n    item_b_id: b,\n    winner_id: a,\n    loser_id: b,\n    response: 'unsure',\n    presented_left_id: a,\n    presented_right_id: b,\n  }\n}\n\nfunction optionGroup(pairings: Array<Record<string, unknown>>, extra: Partial<RankingEvidenceGroup> = {}): RankingEvidenceGroup {\n  return {\n    group_type: 'alternative',\n    criterion_id: null,\n    pass_index: 1,\n    item_ids: [1, 2, 3],\n    question_budget: 3,\n    ranking_target: 'full',\n    pairings,\n    ...extra,\n  }\n}\n\ntest('empty evidence is None', () => {\n  const empty = computeRankingConfidence({})\n  assert.equal(empty.level, 'none')\n  assert.equal(empty.value, 0)\n  assert.equal(empty.label, 'None')\n  assert.deepEqual(emptyRankingConfidence().level, 'none')\n})\n\ntest('six-level boundaries', () => {\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0), 'none')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.01), 'low')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.2), 'low')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.21), 'moderate')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.4), 'moderate')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.41), 'fair')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.6), 'fair')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.61), 'good')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.8), 'good')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(0.81), 'strong')\n  assert.equal(confidenceLevelFromValue(1), 'strong')\n})\n\ntest('unsure does not inflate coverage', () => {\n  const decisive = computeRankingConfidence({\n    optionIds: [1, 2, 3],\n    evidence: [optionGroup([winner(1, 2), winner(1, 3)])],\n  })\n  const withUnsure = computeRankingConfidence({\n    optionIds: [1, 2, 3],\n    evidence: [optionGroup([winner(1, 2), winner(1, 3), unsure(2, 3)])],\n  })\n  assert.equal(withUnsure.value, decisive.value)\n})\n\ntest('undo drops the score', () => {\n  const two = computeRankingConfidence({\n    optionIds: [1, 2, 3],\n    evidence: [optionGroup([winner(1, 2), winner(1, 3)])],\n  })\n  const one = computeRankingConfidence({\n    optionIds: [1, 2, 3],\n    evidence: [optionGroup([winner(1, 2)])],\n  })\n  assert.ok(two.value > one.value)\n})\n\ntest('missing factor ranking keeps multi-factor below Strong', () => {\n  const optionsOnly = computeRankingConfidence({\n    optionIds: [1, 2, 3],\n    evidence: [optionGroup([winner(1, 2), winner(1, 3), winner(2, 3)])],\n  })\n  const multiMissingFactors = computeRankingConfidence({\n    optionIds: [1, 2, 3],\n    factorIds: [10, 11],\n    evidence: [optionGroup([winner(1, 2), winner(1, 3), winner(2, 3)], { criterion_id: 10 })],\n  })\n  assert.ok(optionsOnly.value > multiMissingFactors.value)\n  assert.notEqual(multiMissingFactors.level, 'strong')\n})\n\ntest('color interpolates danger toward success', () => {\n  const low = interpolateConfidenceColor(0)\n  const mid = interpolateConfidenceColor(0.5)\n  const high = interpolateConfidenceColor(1)\n  assert.match(low, /--pc-danger/)\n  assert.match(mid, /--pc-warning/)\n  assert.match(high, /--pc-success/)\n  assert.equal(computeRankingConfidence({}).color, low)\n})\n\ntest('live overlay replaces the current group and undo follows pairings', () => {\n  const seeded: RankingEvidenceGroup[] = [optionGroup([winner(1, 2)], { client_group_id: 'g1' })]\n  const live: RankingEvidenceGroup = optionGroup([winner(1, 2), winner(1, 3)], { client_group_id: 'g1' })\n  const merged = mergeLiveEvidence(seeded, live)\n  assert.equal(merged.length, 1)\n  assert.equal((merged[0].pairings || []).length, 2)\n  const undone = mergeLiveEvidence(merged, optionGroup([winner(1, 2)], { client_group_id: 'g1' }))\n  const withLive = computeRankingConfidence({ optionIds: [1, 2, 3], evidence: merged })\n  const afterUndo = computeRankingConfidence({ optionIds: [1, 2, 3], evidence: undone })\n  assert.ok(withLive.value > afterUndo.value)\n})\n"},"output":"Wrote file successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"filepath":"/workspaces/powerchoice/client/utils/rankingConfidence.test.ts","exists":false,"truncated":false},"title":"client/utils/rankingConfidence.test.ts","time":{"start":1787313772293,"end":1787313772329}},"id":"prt_02433c0fc001Xf4noz11sriPUX","sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","messageID":"msg_02432a9a6001Rl7b09RobsLcXO"}}
{"type":"tool_use","timestamp":1787313780740,"sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","part":{"type":"tool","tool":"write","callID":"call-8bc0100c-70ba-4a7b-91b2-13f7f24f1820-83","state":{"status":"completed","input":{"filePath":"/workspaces/powerchoice/client/composables/useRankingConfidence.ts","content":"import type { SortGroupPayload } from '~/composables/useSortGroupRunner'\nimport {\n  computeRankingConfidence,\n  emptyRankingConfidence,\n  evidenceFromLiveGroup,\n  mergeLiveEvidence,\n  type RankingConfidence,\n  type RankingEvidenceGroup,\n  type RankingEvidencePairing,\n} from '~/utils/rankingConfidence'\n\nexport function useRankingConfidence() {\n  const evidence = ref<RankingEvidenceGroup[]>([])\n  const optionIds = ref<number[]>([])\n  const factorIds = ref<number[]>([])\n  const live = ref<RankingEvidenceGroup | null>(null)\n\n  function seedFromProgress(progress: Record<string, unknown> | null | undefined) {\n    if (!progress) return\n    if (Array.isArray(progress.ranking_evidence)) {\n      evidence.value = progress.ranking_evidence as RankingEvidenceGroup[]\n    }\n    if (Array.isArray(progress.ranking_option_ids) && progress.ranking_option_ids.length) {\n      optionIds.value = progress.ranking_option_ids.map(Number).filter(n => Number.isFinite(n))\n    }\n    if (Array.isArray(progress.ranking_factor_ids) && progress.ranking_factor_ids.length) {\n      factorIds.value = progress.ranking_factor_ids.map(Number).filter(n => Number.isFinite(n))\n    }\n  }\n\n  function setCatalog(options: number[], factors: number[]) {\n    if (options.length) optionIds.value = options.filter(n => Number.isFinite(n) && n)\n    if (factors.length) factorIds.value = factors.filter(n => Number.isFinite(n) && n)\n  }\n\n  function setLive(group: SortGroupPayload | null | undefined, pairings: RankingEvidencePairing[] | undefined) {\n    live.value = evidenceFromLiveGroup(group, pairings)\n  }\n\n  const confidence = computed<RankingConfidence>(() => computeRankingConfidence({\n    evidence: mergeLiveEvidence(evidence.value, live.value),\n    optionIds: optionIds.value,\n    factorIds: factorIds.value,\n  }))\n\n  return {\n    seedFromProgress,\n    setCatalog,\n    setLive,\n    confidence,\n    empty: emptyRankingConfidence(),\n  }\n}\n"},"output":"Wrote file successfully.","metadata":{"diagnostics":{},"filepath":"/workspaces/powerchoice/client/composables/useRankingConfidence.ts","exists":false,"truncated":false},"title":"client/composables/useRankingConfidence.ts","time":{"start":1787313780523,"end":1787313780606}},"id":"prt_02433d713001v2b2VPHkzJ2HDk","sessionID":"ses_fdbd2e5c0ffeIGb90O3O1UoOdD","messageID":"msg_02432a9a6001Rl7b09RobsLcXO"}}
